He had chosen a poor escape route: a hobby group occupied every chair, and a grandfather was demonstrating how to skip between flowerpots without stepping on his seedlings. Cassian came from the other direction. The man stopped.","“You forgot your copy,” Mara said, holding out his corrected proof. “And took Peta's folder.”","He claimed a merger of publicity and research required central control. Peta, arriving behind them, called that nonsense. His manufacturing of certainty could damage future work. Her mitochondrial results did not support a river-wide recovery claim. The folder included samples from threatened species, and their protection depended on accurate reporting, not a favorable portrait of donors.","The funding body had paid for an interdisciplinary fair, not a fight. Its chair wanted a viable way forward. Mara suggested a brief corrected presentation and a separate explanation of how the error occurred. The just-released results would remain available. In the wake of the correction, visitors could judge the long-running project on its actual evidence.","The ex-editor refused. He had trouble explaining why the final file bore his account name. He did not, however, have trouble threatening volunteers. “There will be heads rolling,” he said.","“No,” Cassian replied. “There will be questions answered by the people who made decisions.”","The focus-group coordinator was a mature woman who had once worked in physical therapy. She rejected the idea that asking for clarity showed disloyalty. She had reacted favourably to the project because it let young people ask questions. She was not in favor of turning their curiosity into obedience.","A smoking sign beside the door had been covered by a poster. The ex-editor lit a cigarette. The caretaker asked him to stop. He did not. A reflex made Mara reach for the folder when ash fell; Cassian took the cigarette tray and set it safely away. The caretaker called security. His negligence might not be a crime in itself, but nobody needed to test whether dry paper would burn.","The donor accused the fair of cruelty towards an honored guest. Rowan, who had arrived late, looked from the guest to the cigarette. “Your definition could use expansion.”","The donor promised to respond favorably to a correction if the wording stayed discreet. Mara declined to inflate certainty merely to protect feelings. His long-standing arrangement with the ex-editor was earning more attention now. The man's résumé might impress him, but a reputation could not make a false headline accurate. Overwhelmingly, the committee supported Peta.","Mara hid a smile. Cassian looked extravagantly innocent. As a gift-giver, he had brought a dragon, not a peace treaty. He was willing to use the beast as a privacy screen while Peta checked the folder. The awkward rescue gradually restored her composure.","The chair returned them to the legislative hall. This was a typical administrative error followed by a less typical refusal to correct it, she said. They would put into practice their publication rules. No donor could inhibit the process through threats. The fair might reshape its opening schedule, but it would stick to the evidence.","His abrupt exit had left one suitable question unanswered: who authorized the change? The chair discussed it at length with the secretary. The revised procedure needed to fit the fair's bread-and-butter work. Continual checking of every comma would overwhelm staff, but approval of the final claims should be manageable.","The non-Asian volunteer group asked whether the corrected results changed their river trip. Peta explained that the demographic form had no role in the mitochondrial study. The children could still visit; they must not touch poisonous plants or disturb nesting sites. The confusion showed how easily shared design could imply a connection that did not exist. It could also shed light on the importance of labels.","Outside, surveillance cameras confirmed that the delivery pallet had broken before anyone approached it. A survey of the courtyard found another unstable stack. The organizers reduced the workload by closing one entrance. A stagger through narrow passages would be more dangerous than a longer walk. A volleyball club helped move the safe equipment while its captain managed the crowd.","Cassian asked everyone to stay off the loose paving near the entrance. He could overhear two volunteers worrying that another argument would erupt at the door. He reassured them; the ex-editor had left. A shared route was the most easily imaginable source of another bottleneck. People tend to follow whoever moves first, so he led visitors towards the safe side.","The ex-editor finally returned his badge. His former paper's Pulitzer Prize was apparently no protection against being asked to leave a science fair. Peta did not smile until he was gone. Then she smiled beautifully.","“Will it go well?” she asked Mara.","“Better than it was going to.”"],"questions":[],"wordCount":797,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0113\" Cassian sat beneath the dragon while Mara introduced Peta. Every child in the room looked delighted by the sight of a powerful man trying to avoid being struck by a paper wing. He accepted the humiliation as a constitutional part of marriage.","Peta began with the just-released results. Her mitochondrial work helped identify relationships among samples. It did not reveal the thoughts of animals, certify every corner of the river or predict every future change. A child asked whether dragons had mitochondria. Peta considered the enormous green creature above Cassian and said the paper variety had a different energy problem.","The laughter let her explain the actual question more clearly. She felt honored by the attention. Her mature confidence had returned gradually, and the presentation did not inhibit awkward questions. The funding body would publish the methods alongside the figures. An interdisciplinary team could shed light on a problem without agreeing on every interpretation.","The chair had reacted favourably to the children's suggestions. She would not let the transition to better rules become an excuse for another delay. Up to six final proofs could be checked each week with current staff. That seemed a modest but imaginable target. Cassian offered to help with transport, which he could arrange rather nicely.","The focus-group coordinator asked three pupils to describe what they had changed in the display. One had enlarged the map. Another had separated the unrelated volunteer questionnaire. The third had replaced a drawing of a poisonous plant that looked alarmingly like edible parsley. His attention to detail might yet save someone's picnic.","In the wake of the publicity mistake, the children had learned a useful exercise in checking claims. Their non-Asian volunteer category referred to participating people, not the study animals. No child was required to discuss their own background in front of the room. The coordinator kept that boundary nicely.","The funding body's chair remained in favor of the programme. She wanted a viable review process, not a promise that errors could never happen. It would be put into practice next month. The committee would stick to written approvals and let researchers see the final proofs. Good intentions could not replace those simple steps.","Mara saw the ex-editor's old draft folded in the wastepaper basket. The long-running publicity contract would be reviewed, though that did not decide every future payment. A typical dispute could grow into a war of personalities. The chair refused that invitation. Her response was more modest and more useful: name the correction, protect the records and improve the next publication.","The ex-editor was still entitled to answer the questions in writing. Nobody proposed judging every project he had ever touched. People tend to remember an embarrassing quarrel more vividly than a corrected chart; the chair wanted both recorded fairly. The committee's purpose was to improve the next decision, not to perform the last dispute forever.","Cassian watched Mara listening and had trouble hiding his impatience to leave. She saw it and deliberately asked another question. He responded by lifting the dragon's head over her shoulder. The creature appeared to overhear every word. A child laughed so hard he nearly slipped from his chair. Cassian lowered it before the laughter could erupt into a whole-room mutiny.","At last the legislative hall emptied. The chair thanked Mara; the coordinator hugged Peta. Volunteers moved the smaller displays into storage. The dragon required four people and a doorway negotiation. Its gift-giver discovered that generosity could be measured in lifting rather than money. He did not complain, although Rowan suggested a radically smaller wedding anniversary present.","“Jewellery,” Rowan advised. “It rarely obstructs a fire door.”","“Your imagination disappoints me,” Cassian said.","Mara laughed. She thought the dragon would reshape their dining room for at least a week. Its arrival would not please the housekeeper. But she liked the thought of returning home to an extravagantly silly object chosen because Cassian expected her to smile.","They walked out beneath the evening sky. The event's long voyage through preparations had ended in something better than the promised triumph: a room where children could ask honest questions. Peta had not needed to be infallible to be worth hearing. Neither had the volunteers needed to be silent to be loyal.","Cassian took Mara's hand. “Dinner?”","“Only if we can seat our guest.”","He looked back at the dragon waiting in the van and sighed. She rewarded him with a kiss. The creature rode home first class."],"questions":[],"wordCount":749,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0114\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">婚后外传：公爵带龙去科学展</div><h1>A Dragon at the Dinner Table</h1><p id=\"chapter-0114-p1\"><a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-8385\" data-words=\"8385\">The</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-1915\" data-words=\"1915\">corrected</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-3106\" data-words=\"3106\">fair</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-5733\" data-words=\"5733\">opened</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-8731\" data-words=\"8731\">twenty</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-5252\" data-words=\"5252\">minutes</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-4729\" data-words=\"4729\">late</a>. The Missing Hominid","volume":"婚后外传：动物园里丢失的周末","anchor":"chapter-0115","paragraphs":["Mara had been married for six weeks when a turkey stole her husband's breakfast. Cassian watched the bird vanish beneath a bench with his pastry. \"I thought your family had welcomed me.\" \"That was before it discovered your feeding habits.\" She kissed the sugar from his cheek and took his hand. Their invitation to restore a display at the coastal wildlife park had promised a quiet weekend. It had not mentioned the missing fossil.","Dr Moss met them beside the bison field. A male bull stood between the gate and its keeper, peacefully obstructing the morning. Beyond it, rescued cattle grazed near an old ox. \"We need the hominid tooth cast repaired,\" Moss said, \"but somebody has taken the original photograph and replaced it with an invoice.\" The invoice demanded a sum that gave Mara immediate sticker shock. Its sheer arrogance deserved a frame.","The exhibit concerned human beings and their changing relationship with animals. Its sponsor claimed it would benefit mankind. His representative, Rill, had offered to put up a replacement at once. Moss found that suspiciously convenient. Rill expected to sail through the vote. \"Restorers tend to do the checking before they sell a repair,\" Mara said. \"Let us see what is actually broken.\"","Their path passed an ape enclosure. A female youngster watched Cassian solemnly, then copied his folded arms. Mara laughed. \"An informed critic.\" At the neighboring farm, a hen sheltered beneath a chair while a lamb attempted to eat its notice. Moss moved the paper beyond the small teeth. A zebra had hurt itself on loose fencing. The sheep enclosure needed work, but she refused to let an inflexible donor punish the animals by withholding feed.","Near the pond, a frog sat beside the missing exhibit's muddy crate. A crab occupied a shallow touch pool under supervision; a child wanted to know whether its claw had carried off the photograph. Mara showed him the tiny scale of the marks. He accepted the correction and went to watch a butterfly. A giant dinosaur model shaded the water, its painted eye now wearing a web spun by a spider.","A drone hummed overhead. Rill said it monitored welfare. Moss said its camera had recently stopped recording after dark. \"A bug in the software,\" he had called it. A tame hound following the keepers sniffed the empty crate. It was trained to find lost visitors, not stolen papers. Cassian crouched to let it sniff his hand; it deposited a wet paw on his knee and made the marriage's second claim upon his clothes.","Inside the education room, a kitten slept beside a stuffed fox awaiting restoration. The cat had been rescued from the road. An owl in the adjoining rehabilitation room was due to return to the wild. Moss lowered her voice near it. \"A wild mammal is not a pet because somebody wants to cuddle it.\" The kitten ignored this useful distinction and climbed onto Cassian's boot.","The missing photograph had shown an early hominid excavation. A label accurately named Homo sapiens in a later section. Rill wanted to replace both with a glamorous hologram. He spoke of Nobel Prizes as though a donor could buy one beside a naming plaque. Mara asked to inspect the existing display first. Its rear board carried an old folder about the British argument over Brexit, including a UKIP leaflet belonging to the original donor. That history did not establish who had stolen today's photograph.","Moss led them to the aquarium, where a small shark cruised behind thick glass. The fish's feeding record was up-to-date; the electricity bill was still owing. Bills climbed steadily; the hawk clinic lacked ventilation. A polar habitat education project had absorbed the maintenance money without bringing its promised funds. \"The park cannot let the cooling equipment overheat,\" Moss said. \"A notice about imaginary savings will not keep it running.\"","In the clinic, a technician was examining a sample for a possible parasite from a rescued goat. A single bacterium visible in a teaching photograph had been printed on Rill's warning leaflet as proof that the entire park was unsafe. The technician had supplied no such finding, nor any virus result. Mara photographed the false attribution. Outside, a mosquito found her wrist, and a fly made an ambitious attempt on Cassian's remaining toast.","The keepers had reasonable complaints of their own. Rill proposed replacing their clinic benefit with health-savings plans while reducing paid hours. Moss feared the loss of experienced staff would be a contributory cause of neglect. A favourable inspection could not be obtained by starving the operation that made it possible. \"At the end of the day, we look to people who know the animals,\" she said. \"Not a logo.\"","A guide arrived on horseback to report that the eastern service gate had been opened overnight. His horse had stopped near a deer shelter, where someone had dropped a camera battery. A crow was trying to lift it. Mara recovered the battery before the bird could swallow anything. A keeper returned a dove to its aviary. None of these creatures had arranged the invoice, but somebody wanted their behavior to drown the human questions.","Rill caught up with them beside the gardens. He stood beside a camel, his coat immaculately clean. He wanted Moss to resign, the trustees to accept his emergency contract, and Mara to make up a reassuring description. \"Safety is noncontroversial,\" he said. \"Then the evidence should be easy to discuss,\" she replied. Their positions were polar opposites. Cassian absently straightened her collar, hiding a smile at the quiet force in her voice.","Mara asked why the contract included the old cricket lawn. Rill said the land was useless. Moss had intended to sow a meadow there so insects could thrive. A bee worked a flower beside them. Farther along, a rat had worried open an improperly closed feed sack; a worm moved through its damp underside. Poor storage needed correction. It did not justify selling half the grounds.","A bat emerged from its box as evening approached. The reptile keeper finished securing the enclosure of a snake while visitors watched salmon through a stream window. On the exit path, a squirrel dropped a seed onto Rill's spotless sleeve. He swore. Mara remembered the dust on the empty crate and looked at his clean cuffs again. A man could visit a field without walking through its mud, and vice versa. She had found a question worth asking."],"questions":[],"wordCount":1075,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0115\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">婚后外传：动物园里丢失的周末</div><h1>1. A Tube Full of Trouble","volume":"婚后外传：动物园里丢失的周末","anchor":"chapter-0116","paragraphs":["At dawn, Cassian found his wife under the dinosaur. She lay on a mat, examining its rear support, while a turkey inspected her boots. \"An unflattering rival,\" he said. \"It brought me a feather.\" \"I brought coffee.\" Mara slid out, kissed him, and declared the contest settled. Beneath the model, she had found a screw from the missing hominid display.","Rill arrived with a contractor who wanted to put up fencing around the cricket lawn immediately. Mara asked him to wait. \"We tend to do repairs in a more useful order: identify the damage, then charge for it.\" He called this obstructive. She called his price sticker shock in a suit. The sheer speed of his proposed sale now interested the trustees.","The drone records should have shown the theft. Ivo, visiting with a repaired power unit, found no software bug. Someone had covered the lens, then flown the machine to a blind corner behind the camel enclosure. The camel's keeper remembered Rill carrying a large tube. A hound had barked, and the cattle had moved toward their feed. The male bison, an impressive bull, had blocked the path of his small electric cart.","The hawk keeper had seen the cart pass. Mara examined its scratched side. The hound put a paw on its wheel. One mark resembled a claw; another held orange paint from the dinosaur. A fox in rehabilitation had never been near it. Nor had the female ape, although a visitor had blamed her because she could open a latch. \"Convenient suspects,\" Cassian said. \"Very poor opportunities to hire lawyers,\" Mara answered. The curator had marked every such accusation as unverified.","The employee clinic supplied a second clue. Its technician had photographed a bacterium for a children's lesson, not diagnosed a dangerous outbreak. Rill had cropped the teaching label from his leaflet. The actual parasite found on the goat was receiving appropriate care. His warning about a virus did not name a test result. He wanted the clinic replaced by health-savings plans and its laboratory records removed before the sale.","Moss would not resign. She showed them the unpaid maintenance bill, still owing despite a recent donation. The cooling unit could overheat if neglected. That failure would affect live animals, not merely the polar habitat display Rill advertised. A favourable review had described the park's care, not approved his construction plan. The contracts and the animals could both thrive only if someone paid for the real work.","They checked the aquarium route. A cleaner remembered a tube balanced beside the shark window while Rill spoke on his phone. Near the crab pool, a child had photographed him standing beneath the owl sign. That image also showed the missing rolled photograph. A frog had occupied the foreground and attracted the child's attention; Rill was the accidental evidence in the background.","A keeper found feed dust in the tube's cap. A rat had opened that particular sack, and a worm had moved through the damp spill. The storage defect was contributory to the trail, not proof that every exhibit was unsafe. Mara followed the dust to the poultry building. A hen had sheltered beside the sleeping pen of a pig during repairs. Under the bedding lay the photograph, folded into a case with a poster of a panda.","The image showed a small repair in the original cast. Rill's expensive replacement lacked that detail because it was a cheap copy. The kitten had climbed into the box before a keeper moved it. A mouse had nibbled its packaging; the damage exposed a modern printed label. He had bought several copies and proposed selling one as a unique historical artifact. Mara understood his urgency. Human beings had constructed the fraud. No mammal behind the park's fences had touched the invoice.","Rill caught her leaving the building and seized the tube. Cassian stepped between them. Mara ducked beneath the man's arm, caught the loose strap, and pulled the photograph free. He ran toward the eastern gate. A mounted guide saw the commotion and turned his horse across the path. Rill veered toward the deer field instead. Mara followed on a borrowed bicycle while Cassian ran along the parallel service lane.","At the turn, a sheep gate narrowed the route. A lamb stood behind it, entirely uninterested in human crime. Rill could not shove the gate aside without entering the pen, and the keeper refused to move it for him. He doubled back past the lion building, where Cassian waited. A loudspeaker announced that the gate was closed. The scene ended with the would-be seller of safety trapped by ordinary safe barriers.","Mara leaned against her husband, breathless. \"Your breakfast thief was more successful.\" \"The turkey had a clearer strategy.\" A bee landed on a flower beside them. In the branches above, a bat settled into its box while a crow watched the guards arrive. Cassian brushed a spider from Mara's sleeve before it could join the witness group. Their quiet weekend was becoming crowded.","The trustee who took the evidence remembered the donor's UKIP leaflet and the correspondence about Brexit. Rill had used those old political papers to suggest that overseas grants were legally impossible. The actual grant notice said nothing of the kind. It was noncontroversial to verify the dates before repeating the claim. A British campaign had not cancelled an independent park's accounts fifteen years later.","At the end of the day, Moss could look to a hearing with actual documents. She would not assume they could sail through without questions. A keeper on horseback brought the recovered tube back from the gate. Children watched salmon in the stream, then asked why a snake could not share their picnic. Mara said its keeper would have views. Near the exit, a squirrel had stolen a seed from the feeding area of a zebra. A wolf lay asleep beyond a second barrier, leaving mankind to deal with its own noisier problems.","That evening, Mara helped sow the meadow. Cassian was less skilled with seeds than with catching thieves, a fact she found deeply favourable to the balance of the marriage. \"Nobel Prizes are not awarded for this,\" he reminded her. \"Neither are kisses.\" She made an exception. The lawn needed steady work, and vice versa: the workers needed a patch of ground where something honest could grow."],"questions":[],"wordCount":1056,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0116\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">婚后外传：动物园里丢失的周末</div><h1>2. Cassian kept his plate at shoulder height. Mara said the posture suited him. He offered her the first bite instead, then drew her behind a screen for a kiss before the guests arrived. \"A supportive husband,\" she murmured. \"With a personal interest in the success of this weekend.\"","The hominid cast stood in its repaired frame, the original photograph safely beside it. Mara had made up no missing historical detail. Every new part was identified. The cheap substitute was displayed separately for the trustees to inspect. A painted ape on the educational panel seemed to watch the comparison. A female keeper set its loose label straight and invited the children to notice what the two objects actually shared.","Rill arrived with a final argument: the park's difficulties proved that public care was impossible. Moss put the budget before him. His commission was larger than the maintenance deficit. Nobody had to win Nobel honors to see the arithmetic, though she did not expect every visitor to love it. A hen laid an egg in the little demonstration yard as he spoke. The achievement received more applause than his claim.","The technician showed the full teaching image of the bacterium. Its caption established its purpose; it had never certified an outbreak. The parasite record concerned one treated animal. Rill's cropped leaflet could not be made true by repeating the word danger. The person who had examined the possible software bug also explained that the drone had worked; its view had simply been covered. Cameras recorded what their lenses could see, and vice versa.","An old UKIP leaflet remained among the archive papers. Mara returned it to its dated folder. It belonged to the donor's political history, not a present order to close a zoo. The trustees could reject Rill's grant claim without pretending that an old British party had organized his current fraud. That limited conclusion was noncontroversial once the actual dates were read aloud.","The missing invoice attachment showed the purchase of several modern copies. A small footprint in the dusty packaging belonged to a kitten, not a fossil expert; the larger mark beside it matched the paw of the hound. Neither had written the price. \"Mankind has retained that privilege,\" Cassian said. \"And a remarkable ability to overcharge itself.\" The trustees laughed. Rill did not. His threat to resign from the donor committee sounded less impressive after they discovered its only useful work had been done by Moss.","A trustee who had been inflexible about a construction deadline agreed to delay it. He would not punish the keepers for reporting defects. An independent inspection and a revised maintenance account would precede any new contract. Mara helped put up the corrected notices. The immediate task was to prevent equipment from continuing to overheat, not to build a spectacular polar attraction while the existing rooms struggled. The grant remaining available gave Moss something practical to work with.","She felt sheer relief rather than triumph. The land would not be sold at a false emergency price. Enough money was recovered to make the first repairs, though nobody expected donations to replace sustained care. Visitors experiencing sticker shock at the forged invoice were invited to compare it with the real figures. The collection grew steadily. A small donation could help; pretending it solved every problem would help Rill's next argument.","After the hearing, the family tour took a quieter route. The bison bull had moved away from its gate, allowing the cattle keeper to complete the new barrier. The ox enjoyed brushing. A distant lion slept. A sheep watched while its lamb discovered the pleasure of resting in shade. Beside the camel yard, Mara absently rubbed a smear of dust from Cassian's sleeve. The guide offered a short ride on horseback along the restored path. Cassian accepted, then discovered his wife had requested the calmer mount for him.","\"You continue to amaze me,\" he said.","\"I did promise to take care of you.\"","They stopped by the aquarium. A crab lifted its claw toward a child's moving finger on the outside of the glass. In the larger tank, a shark passed without interest in human finances. At the pond, the frog had returned to its sunny stone. A cricket chirped beneath the bench. The animals were not applauding the investigation; they were continuing their lives, which was what the work had been for.","Moss brought the release plan for the owl and an up-to-date record of care. The hawk was ready to leave; the owl needed longer. Its keeper would comply with the appropriate conditions before release. A fox remained in rehabilitation. Being a wild mammal did not mean it could be hurried into the wild for a good photograph. The kitten stayed as a staff pet. A mosquito drove Cassian indoors. Near the reptile room, a worker repaired a screen through which insects had entered. Small, repetitive jobs kept becoming necessary after the dramatic one was finished.","At the farm, the pig had recovered its stolen glove and the poultry staff had recovered their patience. A panda photograph decorated the new fund box; no panda had been imported to improve publicity. In a separate habitat, a wolf slept while visitors moved quietly past. A mouse had been caught in a humane trap near the feed room. The keeper corrected the storage fault before blaming the rat that had followed an easy smell. Beneath the garden soil, a worm completed work nobody had offered to sponsor.","Mara knelt to sow the last meadow strip. She brushed a spider from her cuff and watched a dove fly toward the aviary roof. The dinosaur model had lost its web during cleaning, though its makers expected the spider to disagree. The new meadow would not drown in grand plans; Moss had arranged watering and seasonal checks. Human beings tend to do better work when somebody returns for the unglamorous part.","They reached the gate in golden evening light. The park was not perfect. It was alive, and its defenders had room to keep learning. Mara began to realise that she had stopped checking the time. Cassian took her hand. \"Still a quiet weekend?\" \"A little crowded.\" A hen hurried past them, followed by the pastry thief. He lifted the empty plate and smiled. \"Then perhaps we should finally go somewhere the guests do not eat my breakfast.\""],"questions":[],"wordCount":1079,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0117\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">婚后外传：动物园里丢失的周末</div><h1>3.
